# Building blocks

The [arithmetic subject index](arithmetic-subject-index.md) links the cutoff probability, successor/division and phase results to their analytic consumers, with proved and refuted scopes kept explicit.

Results used in the study of prime-counting errors, with their formal verification scope stated individually. No originality or first-formalization claim is made. Throughout, $\gamma$ is the Euler–Mascheroni constant.

The Lean umbrella also imports scalar algebraic models and conditional system interfaces. Names containing `operator`, `Fredholm`, `system`, or `evaluator` do not by themselves construct the corresponding analytic operator or discharge the structure fields. Theorems with an explicit `h_rh`, `sys`, or evaluator argument retain that premise. In the current formal threshold chain, the unconditional cumulative zero-free result reaches height $7.465$; extensions above it require the stated deficit, disparity, surplus, or high-frequency evaluator hypotheses. No complete signed cofactor bound is proved.

## Full cutoff probability and conditional dependence

The [two-history allocation identity](prime-distribution/prime-allocation-critical-identity.md) derives the complete arithmetic $W$ from a positive Euler prior with its exact endpoint and continuous density terms. The [prime-score](prime-distribution/prime-score-negative-covariance.md) theorem also proves an upper-orthant product and exponential-moment comparison, while its exact cutoff-$570$ example disproves full negative association. The prime-score and [density-prime](prime-distribution/density-prime-covariance.md) covariance theorems have opposing contributions. Their [combined balance](prime-distribution/combined-prime-density-covariance.md) proves leading compensation but leaves the decisive remainder unsigned. An [exact conditioned counterexample](prime-distribution/conditional-prime-dependence.md) shows why full-law negative pair covariance does not persist on every prime-revelation branch. These are written proofs, with rational certificates for the stated finite checks, and no Lean formalization claim.

## A formal negative dyadic divisor average

The [renewal and dyadic-average note](factorial-and-renewal/division-renewal-dyadic-average.md) proves $\sum_{d=1}^N[\psi(2N/d)-\psi(N/d)-N/d]<-N(3/2-\log4)$ for every integer $N\ge1$. The actual prime-power sum, its factorial identification and the strict margin are formalized in Lean. Distinct written proofs retain the causal renewal, exact dyadic boundary cancellation and signed transfer; none supplies the missing critical two-history sign.

## An actual arithmetic sign sufficient for RH

The [eventual-sign criterion](zeta-and-zeros/actual-critical-sign-criterion.md) proves in Lean that eventual nonpositivity of the explicitly defined, complete arithmetic sum $W(x)$ implies full mathlib RiemannHypothesis. Its Mellin identity, Landau theorem, continuation and pole exclusion are proved, including multiple zeros. The eventual sign itself remains unproved.

The [exact-horizon signed smooth-energy theorem](prime-distribution/signed-smooth-exact-horizon-energy.md) proves an unconditional lower bound for the $c(\log N)^2$-smooth Möbius prefix energy: $\log(Z_N(F_y)/N)\ge(1+\log2+\frac12\log c+o(1))\log N/\log\log N$. For each fixed $c>e^{-2}/4$, RH is equivalent to eventual nonpositivity of the full-minus-smooth energy transport. This is a written analytic proof; the transport sign itself is unproved, and this theorem is not formalized in Lean.

## Centered Goldbach pairs as a multiplicative spectral norm

The [Matsumoto-Suzuki centered Goldbach form](goldbach/goldbach-matsumoto-suzuki-centered-form.md) rewrites the complete centered heat square as a rank-one functional of the reflected zero sum, gamma correction and exact endpoint. It also proves why ordinary Goldbach existence and representation lower bounds control the uncentered positive term rather than the cancellation needed to upper-bound the centered square. [CenteredGoldbachTruncatedHeat.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CenteredGoldbachTruncatedHeat.lean) compiles the finite heat square, cutoff support, interior prime/$\psi$ coefficient and ordinary successor difference with every endpoint retained. The [exact finite scale-increment audit](goldbach/centered-goldbach-heat-scale-increment.md) and [CenteredGoldbachHeatScaleIncrementFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CenteredGoldbachHeatScaleIncrementFinite.lean) expand the literal $T\mapsto2T$ shell into prime-prime, two centering-marginal, and pair-count packets, retain its even/odd split and every prime power, and isolate why Goldbach positivity does not give the required upper comparison.

The [spectral bridge](goldbach/goldbach-multiplicative-spectral-bridge.md) uses the Mellin--Barnes beta identity to express the Dirichlet series of centered additive pairs as a positive gamma-weighted vertical-line norm of $-\zeta'/\zeta-\zeta$. The identity is unconditional in the absolutely convergent range $\sigma>2$; finiteness of the positive spectral norm for every $1<\sigma<2$ is equivalent to RH. The [scaling-kernel and hard-wall audit](goldbach/goldbach-scaling-kernel-hard-wall.md) composes this norm with the actual coherent-core Mellin kernel, retains every prime power through an exact root lift, and quantifies why the required projector degree grows with a subcritical cutoff. These results isolate the exact additive-to-multiplicative estimate that ordinary Goldbach existence or a signed continuation does not provide.

The [one-sided cumulative Goldbach criterion](goldbach/goldbach-one-sided-cumulative-rh-criterion.md) proves that either the upper or the lower $X^{3/2+\varepsilon}$ bound, separately and for every $\varepsilon>0$, on the complete ordered weighted Goldbach cumulative count around $X^2/2$ is RH-equivalent. The same holds for even totals after the exact odd prime-power correction. The converse passes through the positive additive heat square and a one-sided subpower bound for the actual heat response, then uses Landau's theorem. The heat residue also gives unconditional positive and negative $\Omega(X^{3/2})$ oscillations with an explicit small constant; stronger RH-conditional constants are known. Ordinary Goldbach existence supplies only a $t^{-1}$ lower heat-square bound, one power below the $t^{-2}$ main term. [GoldbachCumulativeFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachCumulativeFinite.lean) proves the finite ordered convolution and discrete $\psi$ integral; [GoldbachCumulativeAbel.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachCumulativeAbel.lean) proves the infinite discrete Abel identity; [GoldbachCumulativeStieltjes.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachCumulativeStieltjes.lean) proves the continuous step integral equals the actual positive prime heat square. [GoldbachOneSidedHeatTransfer.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachOneSidedHeatTransfer.lean) and [GoldbachOneSidedLowerHeatTransfer.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachOneSidedLowerHeatTransfer.lean) prove the upper and lower cumulative-bound-to-prime-heat implications. [GoldbachEventualCutoffAdjustment.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachEventualCutoffAdjustment.lean) and [GoldbachEventualUpperCutoffAdjustment.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachEventualUpperCutoffAdjustment.lean) extend eventual inequalities to all cutoffs with explicit constants. The analytic heat Mellin and Landau/RH implications remain written mathematics, with no unconditional one-sided error bound claimed.

RH Agent2's [macroscopic rough-Möbius Goldbach channel](goldbach/macroscopic-rough-mobius-goldbach-channel.md) gives an exact positive-minus-positive decomposition of the actual $\Lambda$ coefficient when $z=X^\alpha\ge\sqrt X$. The [first-residual audit](goldbach/macroscopic-goldbach-first-residual-cancellation.md) derives the universal $-(1+\gamma)X/\log X$ secondary term of the late-prime channel and shows that both its leading and first Goldbach pair terms cancel. The exact remaining source is the centered complete-power measure $d\psi-du$; the late packet observes only its earlier cofactor scales, so its positivity alone cannot provide the RH-scale error. These are unconditional written calculations, not a proof of the needed bound.

The [fixed Suzuki odd-packet criterion](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-odd-continuum-green-and-moving-prime-residual.md) identifies a positive continuum Green term in the odd Weil form and isolates one complete smoothed prime-power discrepancy as the moving signed residual. Its local ordered weighted Goldbach total has an explicit main term of order $X$: either one-sided $O(\sqrt X)$ error bound is separately RH-equivalent, while a Hardy zero forces unconditional fluctuations of both signs at that scale. The residual is also a sign-changing compact filter of the critical-scale Chebyshev error. Its Cesàro square mean is RH-equivalent to boundedness and has an exact zero-sum value under RH. None of these criteria supplies the missing unconditional bound or a Lean proof.

The [Suzuki prime-power onset theorem](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-prime-power-support-spectral-flow.md) tracks the full localized Weil form through each support threshold. A newborn actual prime-power shift has a fixed operator norm but converges strongly to zero; its smooth-trial derivative jump has opposite parity signs. The logarithmic archimedean energy prevents the new shift's boundary-collar eigenvectors from abruptly changing the ground eigenvalue. The resulting local ground-value comparison is $O_n(1/\log(1/(a-(\log n)/2)))$, with no all-window positive margin or Lean formalization.

The [full Suzuki-ground transport theorem](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-full-ground-variational-transport.md) compares the lowest Weil eigenvalue at two windows through the exact pole, gamma, and complete admitted prime-power terms. A min–max sandwich and the strict archimedean drift give a quantitative $O_J(1/\log(1/|a-b|))$ modulus on compact window ranges, including support thresholds. A finite positive reflected-coefficient model crosses zero despite retaining the pole and gamma terms; a normalized symmetric polynomial factor shows the functional equation alone cannot locate zeros. The sandwich and modulus apply to general finite coefficient systems, so the actual $\Lambda$ values yield no all-window sign or RH bound here. This is independently reviewed written mathematics, not Lean formalized.

The [Connes--Weil semigroup obstruction](weil-and-spectral/connes-weil-positive-semigroup-obstruction.md) finds a positive off-diagonal cross form at separation $\log(4/3)$: the exact pole-plus-gamma kernel is $1/(14\sqrt3)$ there, before the first prime shift. For every $\lambda>\sqrt{4/3}$ this violates the form condition for a positivity-preserving heat semigroup. A strictly positive ground-state transform cannot repair the violation. The result excludes this Perron--Frobenius route to a simple even lowest eigenvalue; it does not disprove simplicity or evenness.

The [Connes--Weil gap and residual diagnostic](weil-and-spectral/connes-weil-gap-residual-transfer.md) derives the form-domain angle bound and the operator-domain residual/Temple bounds required to compare the normalized prolate candidate with a simple lowest Weil eigenvector. Its exact Fourier factor on a strip $|\Im z|\le\sigma$ is $((\lambda^{2\sigma}-1)/\sigma)^{1/2}$, with $\sqrt{2\log\lambda}$ on the real axis. Published prolate leakage controls a different operator and supplies no Weil gap or residual. A cofinal lower bound $E_0(\lambda)\ge-o(1)$ would already imply RH by nested-window monotonicity; the residual route remains open.

The [two-vector Weil tail reduction](weil-and-spectral/connes-weil-two-vector-tail-minmax.md) constructs two independent prolate combinations with both exact vanishing conditions and then smooths them into the global radical domain. A harmonic-oscillator compactness argument proves a scale-independent lower bound for their Weil-window Gram matrix. The compact Weil form equals the form of their lower tails; explicit pointwise and weighted $L^2$ Fourier-tail bounds retain the gamma multiplier, both poles and the full prime-power series. A weighted tail of order $\operatorname{poly}(c)e^{-c}$ would now give $E_1\le\operatorname{poly}(c)e^{-2c}$ by min--max. That tail estimate and the corresponding bound for Connes's specific Rayleigh quotient remain unproved.

The [Poisson–Epstein geometry audit](weil-and-spectral/adelic-poisson-epstein-euler-obstruction.md) gives a positive lattice-count theta series with exact Poisson self-duality whose Epstein zeta function nevertheless has infinitely many off-critical zeros. Its finite coefficient witness shows failure of Euler multiplicativity, identifying the actual zeta function's unit local Euler factor as an arithmetic distinction missing from this geometric candidate and from the coherent-weight and symmetric-polynomial countermodels. It compares the result with Connes–Consani–Marcolli's RH-equivalent adelic trace positivity and Suzuki's RH-conditional global factorization. No actual Weil-form positivity or Lean proof follows.

The [Bost–Connes local-occupation audit](weil-and-spectral/bost-connes-weil-local-occupation-diagonal.md) uses the actual Euler factors to build a finite positive prime-translation square $J_a$. On compact tests the complete Weil form is exactly $P+\Gamma+\|J_a f\|^2-2M_a\|f\|^2$, including both poles, the gamma factor and every admitted prime power. For this $J_a$, the signed diagonal is forced: a fixed test narrower than $\log2$ has zero prime correlations while $\|J_a f\|^2=2M_a\|f\|^2\sim4e^a\|f\|^2$. This is a trace-level obstruction to deriving Weil positivity from positive local Gibbs occupations alone, not a BC-to-adelic Hilbert-space map or an RH proof.

The [smooth-frequency boundary-leakage theorem](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-smooth-frequency-boundary-leakage.md) inserts a Fourier cutoff into the actual compact prime-shift operator and keeps its exact boundary commutator, pole and gamma terms. A normalized smooth boundary packet has fixed exterior leakage; the commutator has matching linear-in-$L$ upper and lower scale as the cutoff height tends to infinity at fixed $L$, also on even and odd tests. This rules out treating smooth frequency compression as a norm-small perturbation of the compact-support bound. It does not rule out a certificate that resolves the boundary transition operator or prove Zhu's pointwise-envelope threshold intrinsic to all methods.

The [growing positive boundary-packet space](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-boundary-packet-growing-positive-space.md) retains the exact pole, gamma multiplier, and all admitted prime powers on a $2e^{2L}(1+o(1))$-dimensional subspace that captures a fixed fraction of this boundary commutator. Its compressed prime operator is an exact divisor matrix with norm at most $2L+O(1)$; at frequency $T=\exp(C_\varepsilon+(2+\varepsilon)L)$ the complete Weil form has lower bound $(\varepsilon L/2)\|f\|_2^2$ for every $L>\frac12\log2$. This is a high-frequency signed theorem on a sparse packet space. The resolved-packet argument loses separation below $T\asymp e^{2L}$, and the spaces do not approximate fixed compact tests or imply full Weil positivity.

The [exact successor-cell Weil refinement](weil-and-spectral/successor-cell-weil-refinement-terminal-collar.md) tiles the full window by $I_n=(L-\log(n+1),L-\log n]$ and turns a prime-power left shift into the $d$ consecutive child cells $I_{dn},\ldots,I_{d(n+1)-1}$, with the terminal boundary clipped exactly. The full prime, gamma and pole matrix is explicit, and cell averages approximate fixed smooth tests in the logarithmic form domain; its sign remains open. At the actual prime lag $N=18553$, the hard-window edge retains only a narrow Laplace band near $\sqrt N$, exponentially small for the literal terminal heat source, so the published full-source phase-reversal certificate cannot sign that cell edge.

The [two-collar successor-cell matrix](successor-and-division/successor-cell-even-odd-collar-full-matrix.md) gives the exact full Weil compression on the even/odd boundary block that detects an order-$\sqrt N$ prime eigenvalue. The matching pole entry cancels that leading prime term, and a smaller pole term cancels the leading cross-gamma term. The remaining actual-prime discrepancy has unconditional signed excursions; an eventual lower bound on either parity row is RH-equivalent but unproved.

The [small-measure successor-cell bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-small-measure-positive-space.md) signs every complex vector supported on sufficiently short fixed spatial islands, with all child rows, gamma, and both poles included. Two islands separated by $\log 2$ retain the actual nonzero prime-two edge while their cell spaces fill both intervals and have dimension $\asymp\sqrt N$. The bound is local to small support measure; it gives no sign for the complete dense cell space.

The [successor-cell Weil negative-part bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-part-bound.md) applies to every vector in the full $N$-cell space, with all prime powers, gamma and both poles present. Exact cancellation between the continuous prime density and the pole leaves a bounded-below shifted digamma multiplier; the uniform twisted PNT and the cells' $O(N)$ variation bound control the arithmetic remainder. The resulting negative-part norm is $O(\sqrt N\exp[-c(\log N)^{1/3}/(\log\log N)^{1/3}])$. This error still grows, so it gives no full-space positivity or RH result.

The [classical PNT-rate successor-cell bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-part-vk-density.md) improves that negative-part norm to $O(\sqrt N\exp[-c(\log N)^{3/5}/(\log\log N)^{1/5}])$. It uses the actual all-prime-power PNT error at subexponential Fourier frequencies, then treats the unresolved high frequencies by a coarse/fine split of the exact cells. The allowance remains $N^{1/2-o(1)}$ and gives no RH sign.

The [successor-cell negative-index density bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-index-density.md) proves that the complete $N$-cell Weil form is at least $c\log N$ on a subspace of codimension $O(N/(\log N)^2)$. A rowwise Brun--Titchmarsh estimate, with higher prime powers retained separately, sharpens the prime matrix's Hilbert--Schmidt norm squared to $O(N)$; a low-frequency trace bound handles gamma and one exact rank-one restriction handles the two poles. Thus at most $O(N/(\log N)^2)$ cell eigenvalues are nonpositive. The written result controls their number, not their sign or size, and does not prove RH.

The [fourth-logarithm successor-cell bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-index-fourth-log.md) improves the codimension and nonpositive-eigenvalue count to $O(N\log\log N/(\log N)^4)$, with the same $c\log N$ positive margin. It removes polylogarithmically many coarse cells, treats dilations $d\le\delta(\log N)^2$ in operator norm, and bounds the remaining actual prime-power and continuous matrices in Hilbert--Schmidt norm. Exact pole--continuous-density cancellation retains the full gamma and both pole terms. Only Chebyshev's elementary prime bound is used; the result is written analysis and still permits negative directions.

The [log-log strengthened successor-cell index bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-index-stretched-exponential.md) sharpens the nonpositive-eigenvalue count to $O(N\exp[-c\sqrt{\log N\,\log\log N}])$ while retaining a $c\log N$ positive margin outside the exceptional subspace. It combines high moments of the exact small-prime Fourier multiplier, a separate uniform bound for proper prime powers, low-frequency deletion, and a Hilbert--Schmidt estimate for the remaining large-dilation and continuous-density matrices. The pole--density cancellation is exact, and both gamma and pole terms remain in the complete form. This independently audited written proof controls the number of possible negative directions, not their sign or size; it does not prove RH.

The [successor-cell negative-trace bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-negative-trace-bound.md) also controls their aggregate magnitude: $\operatorname{Tr}(Q_N)_-\ll N\exp[-c\sqrt{\log N\,\log\log N}]$. A variational trace estimate applies the same high moments to the exact small-prime multiplier, while direct nuclear-norm estimates control the large prime powers and the continuous-density matrix on the full cell space. The complete gamma term and both poles remain in the identity. This independently audited written analysis leaves individual signs and RH open.

The [signed residual Hilbert--Schmidt bound](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-successor-cell-signed-residual-hs.md) gives $\|K_{>D,N}-P_N\|_{\rm HS}^2\ll N e^{-c\Phi(N)}$ for $D=\lfloor e^{\eta\Phi(N)}\rfloor$. It uses the unconditional PNT to pair actual prime powers with the continuous density on coarse parent cells and exact one-dilation counting on fine cells, without deleting any cells. The high-parent finite row inequality is [Lean compiled](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuccessorHighParentPrimeRow.lean); the PNT and full matrix estimate remain a written proof. This aggregate signed constraint applies to arbitrary orthonormal families but remains compatible with hypothetical off-line zeros and does not prove RH.

The [causal exponential residual theorem](weil-and-spectral/complete-weil-causal-exponential-signed-cone.md) gives a pointwise signed inequality for the *complete* actual-prime residual on every profile $f(x)=e^{-x/2}h(x)$ with nonnegative nonincreasing compactly supported $h$: $\langle f,(K-P)f\rangle\le0$. It transfers the established all-$x$ bound $\sum_{d\le x}\Lambda(d)/d\le\log x$ through the exact autocorrelation, retaining every prime power. For $f_L=e^{-x/2}\mathbf1_{[0,L]}$, the residual tends to $-2\gamma$ and the full Weil value, including both poles and gamma, tends to $2+\gamma-\log(4\pi)>0$. This is written form-domain analysis, not a Lean theorem or positivity on arbitrary compact tests; an off-line zero remains possible.

The [uniform long-ramp cross theorem](weil-and-spectral/uniform-full-weil-cross-sign-for-long-causal-ramps.md) strengthens the growing-support observation: $Q(f_L,f_M)>0.0086$ for every $\min(L,M)\ge10$, with all actual prime powers, both poles and gamma included. The proof combines the sharp harmonic Mangoldt gap, corrected Johnston--Ramaré--Trudgian bound, an exact polarized residual and a digamma-kernel series; an [Arb certificate](../certificates/mixed_ramp_high_support_certificate.py) checks the strict margin. Every nonzero positive compactly supported mixture of these ramps has a positive complete Weil value. This is an independently audited written theorem, not a Lean theorem or positivity for signed mixtures, and remains compatible with hypothetical high off-line zeros.

The complementary [one-short-ramp cross theorem](weil-and-spectral/complete-weil-cross-sign-for-one-short-causal-ramp.md) proves $Q(f_L,f_M)>0.02647\min(L,M)$ whenever $0<\min(L,M)\le2/25$, with the other length arbitrary. It retains the full actual prime-power and archimedean form. The established harmonic Mangoldt gap signs the polarized residual; a concavity argument for the exact digamma kernel and a [one-sided Arb sum](../certificates/mixed_ramp_small_length_certificate.py) certify the gamma margin. This is an independently audited written result, not a Lean theorem or a sign for the middle range $2/25<\min(L,M)<10$.

The [mixed-ramp transition certificates](weil-and-spectral/certified-mixed-ramp-transition-regions.md) extend complete cross positivity to every $\min(L,M)\ge7$ and to $2/25\le\min(L,M)\le7$ whenever $|L-M|\ge6$. The first proof combines exact prime-power enumeration through $e^{10}$ with the monotone JRT residual envelope; the second uses an exact digamma-tail formula and prime-power enumeration through $e^7$. [Seven-plus](../certificates/mixed_ramp_seven_plus_certificate.py) and [separated-tail](../certificates/mixed_ramp_tail_scalar_certificate.py) Arb scripts check the finite margins. These are written, not Lean, results.

The [consolidated causal-ramp theorem](weil-and-spectral/complete-weil-positive-causal-ramp-cone.md) covers the remaining compact box and proves $Q(f_L,f_M)>0.0024\min\{L,M,1\}$ for every $L,M>0$. Its primary [middle-box certificate](../certificates/mixed_ramp_middle_arb_certificate.py) combines exact prime powers through $e^{10}$ with a JRT lower source above that cutoff and verifies $Q>0.005$ on 56,379 Arb rectangles. An independent [exact-prime corner certificate](../certificates/mixed_ramp_compact_box_certificate.py) uses all prime powers through $e^{13}$ and checks the same box by a different bound. For $f=e^{-x/2}h(x)$ with nonnegative decreasing compact $h$, the positive-mixture representation yields $Q(f)>0.0024\int_0^1 h(t)^2dt$. A [Lean finite-weight audit](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualRampPrimeWeights.lean) checks the actual von Mangoldt summand's monotonicity and exact internal transition endpoint. The complete positivity theorem remains a written and interval-certified copositive result, not a Lean theorem, positivity for signed combinations, Suzuki's RH-equivalent undamped rectangle sign, or an RH proof.

The [signed annular two-bump theorem](weil-and-spectral/actual-signed-annulus-bump-gram.md) proves positivity of the complete actual-zeta Weil form for every complex combination of two separated fixed smooth bumps modulated at frequency $T$, uniformly when their separation satisfies $2w<L\le2\log\log T+c$ and $c<0.712987118284$. The exact cross is a finite prime-power Dirichlet polynomial; PNT bounds its unsigned mass by $e^{L/2}(A_\phi+o(1))$ at growing $L$, and the full gamma and two-pole errors are $o(1)$. Its [Hermitian lower inequality](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualAnnulusGramAlgebra.lean) is Lean-checked; the analytic theorem is written, with no prime-phase cancellation beyond the unsigned bound. It neither covers arbitrary tests of that support nor proves RH.

The [almost-all signed annulus theorem](weil-and-spectral/actual-annulus-almost-all-signed-weil.md), sharpened by [separation Sobolev control and actual-zero-ordinate sampling](weil-and-spectral/actual-annulus-sobolev-zero-sampling.md), gives a complete two-bump Weil margin of at least $(1-\eta)\log X/2$ outside a carrier set and zero-ordinate multiset each bounded by $X\exp[-(1+o(1))\log X\log\log X/R]$, uniformly for every $R\to\infty$ with $R=o(\log X)$. At $R=\kappa\sqrt{\log X\log\log X}$ the exponent coefficient is $1/\kappa$ for every fixed $\kappa>0$. The proof retains all prime powers, gamma and both poles; its analytic estimates are written, while Lean checks only the finite Hermitian algebra. Exceptional off-line zeros remain possible.

The [pointwise off-line microcluster theorem](weil-and-spectral/actual-weil-offline-microcluster-mass.md) applies the fixed-bump complete Weil margin at separation $R=2\log\log T$. If a carrier has no right-half off-line zero in the outer ordinate annulus $(\epsilon_\phi/R,(\log T)^D]$, the central off-line mass satisfies $\sum m_\rho e^{(\beta-1/2)R}\ll_\phi\log T$. Thus, for fixed displacement $\delta>0$, its central microcluster contains at most $O_\phi((\log T)^{1-2\delta})$ zeros with multiplicity. Near-coincident off-line zeros reinforce the negative zero-side term; there is no exceptional carrier set. This is a written zero-side consequence of the already published all-prime-power, gamma-and-pole Weil bound; the finite Hermitian inequality and [reflected-pair sign algebra](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualWeilZeroPairAlgebra.lean) are Lean-compiled. The analytic zero sums are written only. A single off-line zero or an annular companion remains possible, so no RH result follows.

The [chirped Weil-packet theorem](weil-and-spectral/chirped-prime-annulus-constellation.md) signs the complete actual-zeta form on any constellation of $O(\log T)$ disjoint, pole-null chirped translates spanning at most $\alpha\log T$ for fixed $\alpha<2$, with minimum Gram eigenvalue at least $\log(T/(2\pi))-O(1)-o(1)$. The proof uses exact prime-power incidence, smooth autocorrelation decay, gamma localization and an $O(1)$ prime row sum. It is a written microlocal geometric result, not fixed-profile phase cancellation or RH.

The [critical-span extension](weil-and-spectral/chirped-critical-span-constellation.md) reaches center spans through $2\log T-B$ with a fixed additive deficit: for any prescribed $\varepsilon>0$, its complete Weil form is at least $(1-\varepsilon)\log T$ times the coefficient norm. The complete prime-power row estimate, gamma localization, and pole cancellation are written analytic arguments; [CriticalChirpedGramFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CriticalChirpedGramFinite.lean) compiles the finite Hermitian row-budget inequality. The packets still depend on $T$ and have negligible loading at a selected zero at their nominal carrier, so no off-line zero is excluded.

The [reciprocal-prime high-cofactor estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-reciprocal-prime-high-cofactor-saving.md) proves a uniform Vaughan/van der Corput power saving for $\sum\Lambda(n)W(n/D)e(FD/n)$ when $D^\kappa\le F\le D^{1-\kappa}$, including prime powers and sharp BV cutoffs. A third-derivative upgrade saves $D^{1/30}$ uniformly for $D^{1/2}\le F\le D$. Exact pole-null moments remove the zero Poisson mode of the critical-span chirp; for $N=T^\lambda$ with fixed $2<\lambda<29/14$, the high-cofactor slice $d\le (N/T)T^{\delta_*}$ is $O(T^{-(29-14\lambda)/60})$, below the full Weil budget. The [finite complex-weighted $\Lambda*1=\log$ identity and hard-cutoff transfer](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualPrimeCofactorFutureFinite.lean) are Lean-compiled with standard axioms; the phase saving, Poisson step, and analytic errors remain written. The fixed-small-cofactor row, beginning at $k=2$, still has the original scale, so this partial estimate does not prove RH.

The [proper-cofactor cutoff transfer](weil-and-spectral/actual-proper-cofactor-dirichlet-dual-cancellation.md) now proves a power saving for the combined prime row and truncated proper-cofactor head when $2<\lambda<29/14$. It transfers the saved small-prime prefix to the omitted cofactor tail, with two explicit boundary rows and rapidly decaying errors. It does not bound the prime row separately. The earlier spectral argument was incomplete: the exact dual cutoff depends on each zero, and the weighted errors and complementary zero ranges cannot be discarded.

The [three-dimensional Möbius-tail estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-tail-three-dimensional-saving.md) applies Robert and Sargos' monomial exponential-sum theorem to the exact Poisson expansion of the separate prime row. For \(2<\lambda<29/14\), its dual factor orientation moves the concrete controlled Möbius cutoff from \(H T^{(29-14\lambda)/116}\) to \(H T^{(7-3\lambda)/15}\), with a fixed power saving; a parameterized form reaches \(H T^{(14-6\lambda)/15-\eta}\) for every fixed positive \(\eta\) below that exponent. The [Bourgain exponent-pair refinement](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-tail-bourgain-dual-saving.md) moves the analytic endpoint to \((126\lambda-13)/210\). The [Bordellès trilinear refinement](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-tail-bordelles-saving.md) moves it to \(4\lambda/7\), and the [Cao--Zhai large-sieve refinement](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-tail-cao-zhai-large-sieve-saving.md) moves it to \((3\lambda+1)/6\). The [rectangle-wise Robert--Sargos--Bordellès hybrid](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-tail-robert-sargos-bordelles-saving.md) applies the dual Robert--Sargos orientation near balance and Bordellès after an explicit switch. It reaches \(2(\lambda+1)/5\); the fixed-margin cutoff \(T^{2(\lambda+1)/5-1/420}\) has a power-saving prefix bound. The farther Möbius tail remains unbounded. At the later three-fifths contact, the [critical alias reduction](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-critical-semiprime-alias-dispersion.md) records the mean-square and nonzero-alias geometry of the all-unit split. The exact K=3 free factors range over integers and have local coefficient \(1\). Its [prime-fiber sampling estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-critical-prime-fiber-sampling-saving.md) proves \(\mathcal E_=\ll T^\varepsilon P Q^4\) only after restricting them to the prime-prime subcomponent. The [dual row-entry estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-critical-dual-k-row-entry.md) proves \(|\mathcal G(k,k')|\ll T^\varepsilon Q^3\sqrt P\) under the same prime restriction after \(TT^*\) removes the complementary coefficient. The [one-\(Q\) Poisson endpoint estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-critical-one-q-poisson-endpoint.md) treats the unrestricted integer all-unit residual and uses exact product spacing in the Bombieri--Iwaniec double large sieve to recover the required scale with saving exponent zero. The [smooth determinant-shift estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-low-shift-gram-saving.md) bounds the diagonal and every dyadic determinant block \(H\le P Q^{4/3}T^{-\kappa}\), for fixed \(0<\kappa<\lambda/15\). The Gram saving is \(T^{-3\kappa/2}\); its square-root scale is conditional on controlling the complete Gram. The [double-\(Q\) full-rank estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-double-q-full-rank-gram.md) applies weighted Cauchy jointly in both \(Q\)-variables. It controls the nonzero difference blocks with normalized phase \(F\le Q^{3/2}T^{-\kappa}\), for fixed \(0<\kappa<\lambda/10\), with Gram saving \(T^{-2\kappa}\). The [joint outer/alias spacing estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-double-q-dual-spacing-saving.md) inserts a smooth Gram majorant and proves
\[
\frac{|\mathcal J_G^\chi|}{P^3Q^3}\ll_\varepsilon T^\varepsilon\left(\frac{F}{Q^2}+\frac1{Q^3}\right)
\]
uniformly for \(Q\le F\le Q^2T^{-\kappa}\). The exact quadratic inversion of the outer square-root spacing removes the earlier unit-spacing term and reaches a fixed margin below the alias-integrality endpoint. Higher Gram blocks remain open, so these estimates do not yet bound the full endpoint sum, extend the Möbius prefix, or imply RH.

For the canonical all-unit residue, the [fourfold high-block completion](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-double-q-fourfold-resonance-bound.md) traces the free-factor coefficient back to its exact value (1), reverses the self-dual alias transform, applies the (B)-process in both (PQ)-variables, and completes the two remaining smooth (P)-variables.  It proves
\[
\frac{|\mathcal J_G|}{P^3Q^3}\ll_\varepsilon T^\varepsilon
\left(\frac Q{P^2}+\frac{PQ^3}{F^2}+\frac1Q\right)
\]
for (Q^2\le F\le T^{1-\kappa}).  The error terms save powers, but the leading product-resonance ratio is (Q/P^2=T^{\lambda-2}>1).  The new estimate is therefore stronger than direct determinant counting but still does not control the high blocks.  Its Lean companion verifies only the exponent and remainder algebra; the four transforms and product-resonance count are written and independently audited.

The [localized-divisor additive Fourier estimate](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-localized-divisor-fourier-saving.md) retains the complete positive coefficient-one box from the second K=3 expansion.  For every coprime additive twist it proves
\[
 |\mathcal L(a,M)|\ll_\varepsilon T^\varepsilon
 \left(Q^3/M+M+Q^2\right).
\]
At the natural modulus \(M\asymp Q\), this saves a full factor \(Q\) from box mass and lies \(Q^{-1/2}\) below the endpoint target after the physical modes are restored.  Reducing a physical numerator \(k\asymp P\) against that modulus leaves denominator at least \(Q/P\); even this worst gcd gives total ratio \(P/\sqrt Q=T^{-(\lambda-2)/2}\).  The proof specializes the residue-class and geometric-progression method of Coppola--Laporta to the known \(Q\)-long coordinate.  It is a linear additive-twist theorem; transferring the saving to the curved reciprocal phase with loss below the worst-gcd margin \(\sqrt Q/P\) remains open.  Lean checks only the scale and endpoint ledger.

The [complete localized-history cross sum](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-complete-history-cross-sum.md) partitions the literal real divisor fibers in \(\mu*\log=\Lambda\) and proves that, on every mixed composite, the aggregate ordered off-diagonal cross sum is exactly the negative sum of localized squared histories. Both orientations occur; no individual cross term is asserted to be negative. The K3 all-unit coefficient is \(+1\) at \(pq\), while the zero appears only after restoring the logarithmic future factor and every divisor history. [ActualMobiusHistoryCrossSum.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualMobiusHistoryCrossSum.lean) compiles the finite real identity. For transformed complex amplitudes the relevant formula is Hermitian, and the later double-Q variables have not been identified with a complete product-indexed history family. Full regrouping recovers the original actual-\(\Lambda\) packet and gives no new upper bound.

The [global Möbius long-free-factor reduction](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-global-long-free-factor-reduction.md) first applies \((\mu*\mu)*1=\mu\) pointwise, then partitions the two factors. Direct partial summation keeps the packet amplitude out of Robert and Sargos' unweighted inner variable. For \(d=2(\lambda+1)/5+\delta\), it gives a fixed-power saving on the blocks \(q\asymp T^v\) with \(v\le(3\lambda-2)/5-\delta-\theta\), subject to the displayed positivity and nonempty-range conditions. The [Lean exponent module](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GlobalCOneLongFreeFactor.lean) and its [axiom audit](../formalization/verification/GlobalCOneLongFreeFactorAudit.lean) check the rational loss budget. This is a partial global-partition reduction. The balanced short-free-factor blocks remain open, so the complete pointwise endpoint does not move.

The [three-factor Möbius prefix extension](weil-and-spectral/actual-mobius-k3-bottom-slice-saving.md) works instead inside the original uncollapsed Vaughan term \(b_0*b_0*1\). For every fixed \(0<\kappa<(\lambda-2)/5\), an unequal-aspect K=3 expansion, the Robert--Sargos/Bordellès switch, a buffered subset argument, and the published free-factor pruning theorem control every Type II rectangle through \(d_F=3\lambda/5-\kappa\). The inherited Bourgain Type I estimate remains power saving there, so the complete original Möbius prefix through \(T^{d_F}\) is power saving. An explicit boundary configuration makes all three analytic margins vanish at equality, so this method does not include \(d=3\lambda/5\). The [Lean exponent module](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualMobiusK3BottomSlice.lean) and its [axiom audit](../formalization/verification/ActualMobiusK3BottomSliceAudit.lean) check the support, buffer, Type I, endpoint, and obstruction algebra. The unrestricted global \((\mu*\mu)*1\) partition is a different slicing, and the tail beyond this new cutoff remains open; no RH conclusion follows.

The [Brun–Titchmarsh span extension](weil-and-spectral/chirped-brun-titchmarsh-span-constellation.md) sharpens the same complete actual-zeta inequality to spans $2\log T+2\log\log T-B$; at span $2\log T+O(1)$ it gives the additive margin $Q\ge(\log T-O(1))\|c\|_2^2$. Montgomery and Vaughan's unconditional arbitrary-interval prime bound, together with a separate count of every higher prime power, removes a factor $\log T$ from the large-distance correlation row. The arithmetic and gamma estimates are written; the existing Lean theorem covers the finite Hermitian row implication. This absolute bound still has negligible loading on an individual off-line zero and does not prove RH.

The [chirped prime-cross endpoint identity](weil-and-spectral/chirped-prime-cross-endpoint-conservation.md) gives $\int_0^D e^{-d/2}P_T(d)\,dd=O(T^{-1})$ uniformly in $D$, and the prime number theorem sharpens this to $O(\varepsilon(D)/T)$ with $\varepsilon(D)=\sup_{e^{D-w}\le x\le e^{D+w}}|\psi(x)-x|/x\to0$. The exact finite expression contains only prime powers with $e^{D-w}<n<e^{D+w}$; every earlier prime-power contribution cancels by the packet's pole-null moment. Its convergent first weighted moment equals $-E_{1/2}(u_T)\overline{E_{-1/2}(u_T)}/\|(\partial_x^2-1/4)u_T\|_2^2$, hence is superpolynomially small in $T$. [CriticalChirpedEndpointFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CriticalChirpedEndpointFinite.lean) compiles the exact finite integral, full von Mangoldt weight, strict collar endpoints, and cutoff stability once $N\ge e^{D+w}$. [CriticalChirpedLaplaceFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CriticalChirpedLaplaceFinite.lean) compiles the complex finite Laplace factorization and the finite-transform limit to actual $-\zeta'/\zeta$ for $\Re z>1/2$, retaining every prime power. The full improper-integral identity, packet construction, and analytic estimates remain written. None of these controls $P_T(d)$ at one separation or isolates an off-line zero.

The [successor-weighted zero trace](successor-and-division/successor-mangoldt-zero-trace.md) combines Gonek's unconditional uniform Landau formula with the exact adjacent prime-power parity filter. For $L\to\infty$ and $L\le(1-\varepsilon)\log X-w$, its $+1$-weighted smoothed sum over $X<\Im\rho\le2X$ is $O(X(1+L)e^{-L}+e^L\log X\log\log X)=o(XL)$, whereas the unshifted trace is asymptotic to $-A_\phi XL/(2\pi)$. [SuccessorMangoldtParity.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuccessorMangoldtParity.lean) compiles the exact support and power-of-two weight; the zero-trace estimates are written analytic consequences of Gonek and the PNT. This dyadic average does not exclude individual off-line zeros or prove RH.

The [two-rate Weil plane](weil-and-spectral/complete-weil-two-exponential-rate-positive-plane.md) has exact Gram entries $Q(u_a,u_b)=(F(a)+F(b))/(a+b)$ for $u_a(x)=e^{-ax}\mathbf1_{x\ge0}$, $a,b>1/2$, and $F(a)=\xi'(a+1/2)/\xi(a+1/2)$. Pairing actual zeta zeros, using the rigorous finite-height verification below $3\cdot10^{12}$ and the unconditional critical strip above it, proves the Gram matrix is positive definite whenever $a\ne b$. Thus every complex signed combination of two rates is positive after sufficiently large compact cutoff, with all prime powers, poles and gamma present. The [packet and determinant algebra](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/TwoRateWeilGramAlgebra.lean) compiles in Lean; the analytic identity and zero sum remain an independently audited written proof. This two-dimensional sign still permits a hypothetical high off-line zero and does not prove RH.

The [finite successor-cell Euler transfer](successor-and-division/successor-cell-finite-euler-transfer.md) proves that these left-shift matrices obey $R_aR_b=R_{ab}$ even after the hard cutoff. Their finite operator Euler product has the complete actual prime-power block as its Hermitian logarithmic derivative at $s=1/2$. [SuccessorCellTransferFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuccessorCellTransferFinite.lean) compiles the child-index, cutoff semigroup, prime-two gauge carry, exact collar weight, phase-rigidity laws, and uniform logarithmic and unit-circle carry bounds. A multiplicative sign cannot conjugate doubling past cell eight unless trivial, while a binary-depth phase reverses every power-of-two shift exactly. Under exact simultaneous scalar conjugacy, the 2- and 3-shifts force every diagonal sign or complex unit phase to be trivial already at cell four. The 3-shift has opposite phases on complete columns in every deep dyadic shell, so its defect from any scalar phase has operator norm at least one. The smooth multiplicative phase $n^{it}$ nevertheless gives an approximate spectral translation on high cells, with a bound retaining every active prime power. The product is automatically invertible at every finite cutoff, while its uncentered critical-line norm grows as $\sqrt N$ on the first cell; none of these identities supplies a Weil sign or zeta zero-free claim.

The [Suzuki coefficient variation](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-coefficient-prime-history-variation.md) connects this finite Euler transfer to Suzuki's arithmetic Hankel family. Its first variation at parameter zero is exactly $2\Lambda(n)$; its second is $4(\Lambda*\Lambda)(n)$, whose same-prime part must be subtracted to recover the original distinct-prime $V/W$ coefficient. [SuzukiCoefficientVariation.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuzukiCoefficientVariation.lean) now compiles both derivatives and the finite $\Lambda*\Lambda$ identification for actual mathlib Möbius and von Mangoldt functions. The finite operator ratio remains written mathematics. This is multiplicative history, distinct from additive Goldbach pairs, and supplies no Hankel sign or RH proof.

The [Suzuki–Goldbach pole-normalizer bridge](goldbach/suzuki-goldbach-pole-normalizer-bridge.md) puts the two histories in one exact parameter calculation: the uncentered additive square has both the shifted multiplicative $\Lambda*\Lambda$ endpoint and genuine additive Goldbach pairs, while subtracting the state-one seed isolates the latter. Suzuki's zeta-pole residue has derivative $2$ at zero, so its normalized source has derivative $2(\Lambda-1)$ and the additive square's second variation is eight times the complete centered Goldbach coefficient. [SuzukiGoldbachVariation.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuzukiGoldbachVariation.lean) compiles the finite seed identity and pure additive variation. [SuzukiPoleNormalizer.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuzukiPoleNormalizer.lean) compiles the actual pole-residue derivative and the centered additive square's quadratic limit $C_\omega(N)/\omega^2\to4c_N$, with the exact existing centered Goldbach coefficient and standard axioms. The classical second-derivative wording and infinite heat exchange remain written. The positive square does not bound its signed centered coefficients or establish RH.

The [sparse prime-weight test of the Suzuki–Goldbach bridge](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-pole-normalized-sparse-prime-countermodel.md) preserves complete positive prime-power weights, a stretched-exponential PNT error, factorial-ground positivity, positive Euler-quotient coefficients, first pole normalization and the nonnegative centered Goldbach heat square. A sparse downweighting nevertheless makes the same completed first variation fall below every $-Cx^\delta$, $\delta<1/2$, along a sequence, and gives the centered Goldbach heat energy logarithmic growth exponent exactly $1$. Its [theta-form stability estimate](theta-and-heat/theta-sparse-prime-form-stability-test.md) shows that the full positive jump form keeps the same domain and can be arbitrarily close in bounded-operator norm while that arithmetic excursion persists. [FactorialGroundDownweightFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/FactorialGroundDownweightFinite.lean) compiles its finite ground comparison and actual-von-Mangoldt specialization with standard axioms. The infinite construction is a written countermodel to inference from those qualitative properties, not a deformation of the actual zeta functional equation or a result about RH.

The [balanced three-band companion](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-balanced-prime-bands-pole-ground-countermodel.md) additionally matches the actual factorial-ground limit $1+\gamma$ and the Euler product's right pole residue and constant term. Its exact two-moment cancellation still leaves a negative weighted prime-clock excursion, and, with sufficiently separated bands, its centered Goldbach heat energy also has logarithmic limsup exponent $1$. This is written analysis of a deformed positive coefficient system; it leaves the actual divisor identity and zeta functional equation untouched and proves no RH estimate.

The [theta-convolution null-mode calculation](theta-and-heat/theta-radical-convolution-zero-mode-obstruction.md) shows why the exact radical identity detects that sparse perturbation but cannot by itself invert a hypothetical off-critical zero mode. At a zero of multiplicity $d$, the first polynomial-exponential input surviving the smoothing is $u^d e^{su}$; its complete Weil or affine sign remains open.

The [small-parameter Suzuki Hankel window](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-hankel-small-omega-structural-window.md) proves in written analysis that $\sqrt{x}h_\omega^{\langle1\rangle}(x)\to1$ uniformly for $2\le x\le\omega^{-2}(\log(1/\omega))^A$ for each fixed $A>0$, using the actual $\Lambda$ coefficients, Suzuki gamma kernel, and an unconditional quantitative prime number theorem. Qualitative PNT alone does not control any fixed polynomial scale $x=\omega^{-a}$ with $a>2$ by this argument. The [boundary-layer companion](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-hankel-exponential-boundary-layer.md) proves a stronger $\omega^{-2}\exp(\frac14\sqrt{\log(1/\omega)})$ window and an exact zero-moment identity; this shorter cutoff proof remains an alternate route. The analytic uniform estimates are not Lean formalized and leave the fixed-$\omega$ tails and RH open.

The [centered Suzuki discrepancy](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-hankel-exponential-boundary-layer.md) has the exact first variation $\partial_{\omega+}E_\omega(y)|_0=2(\psi(y)-y)$, so one ordinary counting step recovers $2(\Lambda(N+1)-1)$ with every prime power and the initial endpoint. [SuzukiCoefficientVariation.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/SuzukiCoefficientVariation.lean) compiles the finite prefix derivative $C'_0(N)=2\psi(N)$; the zeta-pole normalizer remains written analysis. The [completed variation obstruction](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-completed-curvature-sign-obstruction.md) proves that both $\partial_\omega R_\omega(x)|_0$ and $\partial_\omega^2R_\omega(x)|_0$ take both signs arbitrarily far out, so positive $\Lambda$ and $\Lambda*\Lambda$ coefficients do not sign the full gamma-and-pole kernel. Neither result settles the fixed-parameter Hankel sign or RH.

The [completed Suzuki first-variation identity](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-first-variation-weighted-prime-clock.md) evaluates $r_1(x)=2\sum_{n<x}\Lambda(n)/\sqrt n-4\sqrt x+\kappa-J(x)$ for noninteger $x$, with an explicit positive $J(x)=O(x^{-5/2})$ and every prime power retained. Abel summation makes $r_1(x)=O(\log^3x)$ equivalent to RH, and Landau gives an equivalent all-$\varepsilon$ one-sided lower bound $r_1(x)\ge-C_\varepsilon x^\varepsilon$; neither growth estimate is proved. The finite Suzuki derivatives and [weighted prime-power Abel identity](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/WeightedPrimePowers.lean) are compiled in Lean; the gamma derivative and analytic criterion remain written.

The [completed Suzuki curvature sign obstruction](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-completed-curvature-sign-obstruction.md) applies that coefficient identity to the actual gamma-and-pole Hankel kernel. Its second parameter variation has negative logarithmic spikes just after every prime-power arrival and positive values arbitrarily far out, proved using the exact completed Mellin transform and Landau's theorem. This is a written analytic result, not yet a Lean theorem; it neither refutes the positive-parameter Hankel sign target nor proves RH.

The [divisor-matrix sharpness addendum](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-divisor-matrix-sharpness.md) evaluates the Rayleigh quotient of $w_n=n^{-1/2}$ by the exact identity $2\sum_{m\le N}(\log m)/m$ and proves that both compressed prime blocks have largest eigenvalue $\log N+O(1)$. [CompactWeilDivisorRayleighFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CompactWeilDivisorRayleighFinite.lean) compiles the finite prime-power factor-pair identity; the matrix and asymptotic steps remain written. No PNT input is needed. This fixes the leading scalar prime cost at $2L$ inside the resolved full-label packet model; it says nothing about the sign of the complete form when those packets overlap.

The [complete divisor-history energy](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-divisor-graph-dirichlet-energy.md) gives an exact ground-state transform of the actual prime matrix: its $\log N$ deficit is a square over every prime-power divisor edge plus a pointwise boundary potential. Balazard's known all-cutoff inequality $\sum_{d\le x}\Lambda(d)/d\le\log x$ signs that potential, so the prime block satisfies $K_N\preceq(\log N)I$. [CompactWeilDivisorEnergyFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CompactWeilDivisorEnergyFinite.lean) formalizes the real and complex finite identities, factor-pair and pointwise boundary regrouping, edge-square sign, and the implication from explicit finite Balazard hypotheses to the complex spectral ceiling. The published proof of the Balazard inequality and the full gamma/pole comparison remain outside that Lean module. A comparison strong enough for arbitrary compact tests remains open.

The [full-label critical packet theorem](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-full-history-critical-positive-space.md) uses complete divisor-history energy to bound the off-center gamma interaction and signed pole uniformly when $T=cN$ for a sufficiently large fixed $c$. An exact moment transfer $(\log N/N)\sum|g_n|^2\le2E+C\sum|g_n|^2/n$ pays mass near the cutoff; weighted Schur then absorbs the gamma row into $E$, while the pole costs a further fraction of $E$ plus bounded mass. The resulting full Weil form is positive on all $2N-1$ resolved packets, including every middle divisor label. This is a written proof, not Lean formalized beyond the finite real graph identity. The packet spaces stay sparse in fixed spatial intervals, so this does not establish positivity for all compact tests or RH.

The [critical packet crossover](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-critical-packet-crossover.md) sets $T=cN$ with fixed $c>4$ and retains the signed gamma and pole couplings. On each fixed continuous log-shell profile $h$, the complete form divided by $(\log N)^2$ tends to $\int_0^1|h|^2-|\int_0^1h|^2$. On the normalized constant profile, where this leading form vanishes, the finite residual tends to $\log c+\gamma+g_p+\mu_p^2/c>0$; the off-center gamma term contributes $-\mu_p^2/c$ and the pole contributes $+2\mu_p^2/c$. This is a critical-scale form limit on fixed profiles, not a uniform bound on the growing packet space or a full-window Weil certificate.

The [two-profile Perron crossover](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-two-profile-perron-crossover.md) keeps both packet profiles at this same $T=cN$ scale. Its complete $2\times2$ Weil matrix has diagonal constants $\log c+\gamma+g_{--}+m_-^2/c$ and $\log c+\gamma+1+g_{++}+m_+^2/c$, and mixed entry $g_{-+}+m_-m_+/c<0$. The $+1$ records deletion of label one from the outgoing prime block; the positive rank-one term is the pole contribution after the negative off-center gamma interaction. The linked matched-score calculation gives an exact projection in the separate killed-theta covariance space, but no bound on its remaining conditional edge energy or RH.

The [critical second-order phase tests](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-critical-packet-second-order-tests.md) show that a fixed smooth mean-zero perturbation at amplitude $(\log N)^{-1/2}$ adds its $L^2$ mass to the positive Perron residual. Fixed finite-prime multiplicative phase twists retain prime Rayleigh energy $\log N-O(1)$, but their exact full-form residual is $\log c+\gamma+g_p+D_\chi+(\mu_p^2/c)|\kappa_\chi|^2>0$, with the gamma and pole phase correlations computed rather than bounded absolutely. The top-half and early-label endpoint blocks have growing positive margins. Mixed vectors with $N$-dependent prime phases remain open; individual phase positivity does not prove positivity of their span.

The [separated divisor-band theorem](weil-and-spectral/compact-weil-separated-divisor-bands-positive-space.md) keeps all labels $n\le N^\alpha$ together with the top half $N/2<n\le N$ at the same critical frequency $T=cN$, where $0<\alpha<1$ and $c>4$. It retains every admitted prime power, both pole moments, and the full gamma matrix. The low-band prime cost is $\alpha\log N+O(1)$; the top band has no internal prime edge and its off-center gamma loss is at most $(4\delta/c)\log N+O(1)$. The prime and pole cross blocks are $O(\sqrt{\log N})$, yielding a positive margin of order $\min(1-\alpha,1-4\delta/c)\log N$ on a space of dimension $N+2N^\alpha+O(1)$. The omitted middle labels and full-window sign remain open.

The [short-interval odd graph theorem](prime-distribution/odd-prime-graph-short-interval-linear-log-gap.md) uses Guth–Maynard's uniform prime asymptotic to control the actual all-prime-power shift pairing for every $H_0^1$ test with derivative ratio at most $e^{\tau a}$, for fixed $\tau<13/15$. Fourier truncation then gives a leading $e^a$ graph gap for odd tests whose normalized logarithmic energy is $o(a)$, with quantitative loss $C\sqrt{(\mathcal L[F]/\|F\|_2^2+1)/a}$. The signed pairing may still dwarf the archimedean cost; Weil positivity and RH remain open. These are written proofs, not Lean formalizations.

The [zero-free-region odd graph theorem](prime-distribution/odd-prime-graph-zero-free-region-log-frequency-gap.md) uses the actual $-\zeta'/\zeta$ and the Korobov–Vinogradov region to cancel the prime-minus-continuum Fourier multiplier uniformly up to $|t|\le\exp(K a^\vartheta)$ for each fixed $\vartheta<3/2$. Fourier-tail control extends the leading $e^a$ odd graph gap to every family with normalized logarithmic energy $O(a^\vartheta)$; a positive $e^a$ prime pairing must exceed that energy scale. This strengthens the growing-window graph conclusion without proving the much finer signed bound needed for Weil positivity or RH. It is a written analytic proof, not Lean formalized.

RH Agent2's [complete early-divisor sieve](prime-distribution/large-prime-additive-sieve-and-indefinite-kernel.md) isolates ordinary primes above $\sqrt X$ and shows that the additive triangle kernel is indefinite even on those prime coordinates. The [critical correction](goldbach/high-prime-goldbach-critical-correction.md) proves that removing small primes and all proper powers changes the complete cumulative Goldbach count by $(10/3+o(1))X^{3/2}$, with prime squares contributing $4/3$ after the ordered-pair factor. The [cutoff phase transition](goldbach/high-prime-goldbach-cutoff-phase-transition.md) gives correction constants $4/3$ below $\sqrt X$, $10/3$ at $\sqrt X$, and leading order $2X^{1+\alpha}$ above it; at $z/\sqrt X\to c$ the critical constant is $2c+4/3$. These unconditional localizations transfer the one-sided RH criteria to shifted ordinary-prime pair sums, but prove none of the required one-sided bounds. The exact sieve projector applies only at cutoffs $z\ge\sqrt X$.

The [complete prime-history phase twist](goldbach/goldbach-complete-prime-phase-cut-energy.md) flips each generator $p\le c\sqrt X$ and retains its parity on every power. Its exact ordered Goldbach difference is a nonnegative additive-graph cut energy, and the difference from the untwisted complete Mangoldt count is $(4c+o(1))X^{3/2}$, including the even-sum restriction. A fixed finite generator set costs only $O_S(X\log X)$. The deterministic positive cut cannot sign the centered Goldbach error or prove Goldbach existence; the moving-set result is distinct from fixed-prime Weil packet positivity.

The [complex prime-phase square transition](goldbach/goldbach-complex-prime-phase-square-transition.md) extends this cut to a Hermitian Goldbach pair with a unit phase on every generator below $c\sqrt X$. The complete prime-square history contributes another $X^{3/2}$ term: the limiting cut coefficient is $2c(1-\cos\theta)+2g(c)(1-\cos2\theta)$, $g(c)=\min(c,1)-\min(c,1)^3/3$. The maximizing phase is nonreal for $c<8/3$ and the sign flip takes over at $c=8/3$; the same leading law holds for even totals. [GoldbachPrimePhaseCutFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachPrimePhaseCutFinite.lean) compiles the complete finite real-angle cut identity and positivity with actual Mangoldt weights. The arithmetic asymptotic remains a written PNT proof, and the cut does not bound the centered Goldbach error.

The [centered phase first-harmonic bridge](goldbach/goldbach-phase-first-harmonic-prime-error.md) projects the complete Hermitian Goldbach triangle onto angular frequency one. Its exact finite formula retains every prime-power interaction and reconstructs $E(N)=\psi(N)-N$ as $\mathfrak H_1(N,c\sqrt N)/(2\vartheta(c\sqrt N))+O_c(\sqrt N\log N)$. Hence an all-$\varepsilon$ $O(N^{1+\varepsilon})$ bound for this signed harmonic is RH-equivalent, but no such bound follows from Goldbach existence or cut positivity. `centered_phase_difference` in [GoldbachPrimePhaseCutFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachPrimePhaseCutFinite.lean) compiles the exact finite center cancellation; the angular extraction and analytic equivalence remain written proofs.

The [even-total three-phase harmonic](goldbach/goldbach-even-three-phase-prime-error.md) puts the single generator $3$ on the parity-aware odd-lattice reference $r(n)=2\mathbf1_{n\text{ odd}}$. It proves the exact identity $\mathfrak H^{\rm even}_1(N)=2\log3\,[E(N-3)-P_2(N-3)-1-(\log3)d_N]$ for even $N$, where $P_2$ retains all powers of two and $0\le d_N\le2$ counts the uncanceled $3^k$ ladder. Thus an all-$\varepsilon$ $O(N^{1/2+\varepsilon})$ bound for this signed even-total harmonic is RH-equivalent; neither Goldbach existence nor raw coefficient positivity proves it. [GoldbachEvenPhaseCenterFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachEvenPhaseCenterFinite.lean) compiles the finite parity-centered phase algebra. [GoldbachThreePhaseHarmonicFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachThreePhaseHarmonicFinite.lean) compiles the three-adic row classification and $d_N\le2$. [GoldbachThreePhaseEndpoint.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachThreePhaseEndpoint.lean) compiles the exact arithmetic identity, including the $N-2$ endpoint and every power of two. [GoldbachThreePhaseFourier.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachThreePhaseFourier.lean) compiles the original complex phase, the real ordered triangle, and its literal first Fourier integral through the exact prime-error reconstruction.

The [literal-successor stability theorem](goldbach/goldbach-three-phase-successor-stability.md) preserves every complete prime-power birth and the clamped label-one reference. The transported three-phase harmonic differs from the original by an explicit $O(\log^2N)$ bound, sharpened to $O(\log N\log\log N)$ using arbitrary-interval Brun--Titchmarsh and separate proper-power counting. [GoldbachThreePhaseTransportFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachThreePhaseTransportFinite.lean) compiles the complex Fourier projection for arbitrary real pair survival factors and the finite positive prefix-average algebra. The actual successor probability and asymptotic bound remain written proofs. This stability does not improve the global $\psi(x)-x$ bound or establish RH.

Classical Littlewood oscillation transfers through that exact formula: the actual even first harmonic is $\Omega_\pm(\sqrt N\log\log\log N)$, so it has no eventual fixed sign. Quantitative sign-change results also give $\gg\log T$ even cutoffs up to $T+3$ where the harmonic and the complete prime error are only $O(\log T)$. [GoldbachOddCrossing.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachOddCrossing.lean) compiles the finite actual two-step bound underlying this sampling; the global oscillation and quantitative sign-change inputs remain written mathematics. These sparse bounds do not prove RH.

The [even-centered 118 counterexample](goldbach/goldbach-even-centered-118-counterexample.md) proves $C_{\rm even}(118;0)<-25$ for the actual von Mangoldt weights and odd-lattice reference. [GoldbachEvenPhaseCounterexample.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachEvenPhaseCounterexample.lean) compiles a rational-log interval certificate over all 3,481 ordered even pairs. This rules out global nonnegativity of the parity-centered cumulative triangle; it says nothing about an eventual sign or the still-open RH-scale harmonic bound.

The [Suzuki Riesz–factorial-ground Volterra bridge](weil-and-spectral/suzuki-riesz-factorial-ground-volterra.md) rewrites Suzuki's RH-equivalent weighted-prime sign signal exactly as a positive twofold Volterra primitive of the actual $F$ minus its terminal ground value and the elementary $\log x+4$ term. Positivity of $F$ gives a strict negative exponential average at every finite horizon for all real damping $z\ge1/2$, including an explicit terminal formula and the total moment $-16-4\gamma$ at $z=1/2$. An abstract positive-ground bump reverses the averaged sign below that damping threshold. The pointwise Suzuki sign and any RH-level bound remain open; this is written analysis, not Lean formalized.

The [divisor-length truncation barrier](prime-distribution/sqrt-divisor-truncation-parity-barrier.md) exhibits actual semiprimes $pq$ whose complete early-prime history cancels but whose short divisor sum is $\pm1$. Their selected ordered cross with genuine large primes has absolute size asymptotic to $X^2/(108\log X)$ for every fixed sublinear-power divisor cutoff; its sign changes at the square-root length. This refutes an absolute-error replacement of complete histories by a short divisor sum at RH scale, without bounding the total signed error.

RH Agent3's [centered Goldbach parity compensation](goldbach/centered-goldbach-odd-parity-compensation.md) proves that the actual centered coefficient is $-k+o(k)$ on every odd total. Its odd heat contribution is $-N/2+o(N)$, while PNT forces the even contribution to be $+N/2+o(N)$, with matching cancellation on every fixed macroscopic band. This is an unconditional leading-scale signed repair; its $o(N)$ remainder does not prove the RH-equivalent subpower centered-energy bound.

[GoldbachTotalImbalanceFinite.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/GoldbachTotalImbalanceFinite.lean) formalizes the exact finite centered prime-power row identity: the original maximum-kernel row equals twice its additive-total row minus a signed imbalance row, and the additive-total row is the complete centered mass divided by the total. Once the cutoff admits every pair, its mass is exactly the existing centered Goldbach heat coefficient. It retains the atom at one and both cutoff endpoints. The imbalance kernel is pointwise nonnegative but indefinite on signed rows, so the identity supplies no RH bound.

RH Agent3's [complete square-root-sieve theta packet](theta-and-heat/complete-sieve-theta-same-half-prime-bilinear-dyadic-visibility.md) retains every prime power, the ghost backward translation and the full same-half form on a smooth finite embedding. The [compact killed-ground barrier](theta-and-heat/theta-killed-ground-compact-lower-barrier-dyadic-cone.md) proves that the actual ground has positive essential infimum on each fixed compact exterior strip. It makes the complete Möbius cone's $p\to2p$ same-half prime energy $\gg N/\log N$ unconditionally, while its tagged large-prime edges are exponentially attenuated. This is a fixed-core theta-form statement; no original Abel/clock metric comparison or RH bound follows.

In the original factorial metric, RH Agent3's [literal $p$ to $2p$ Picone cross](dynamics-and-feedback/literal-picone-p-to-2p-signed-cross.md) keeps the full future prime-power observation and continuum density. Each separate polarized observation is $-[1+(\log2)/2]\log p/(pF_\infty)+o(\log p/p)$, but their signed difference is $O_A(\log p/(p\log^A p))$ for every fixed $A>0$ and is summable over these prime pairs. The actual process cross is $-\log p/(2p)+o(\log p/p)$ for large $p$; its selected dyadic-pair sum through $Y$ is $-\frac12\log Y+O(\log\log Y)$. This does not bound the full accumulated work.

## Prime-state hard wall and causal renewal criterion

The [prime-state hard wall](prime-distribution/prime-state-hard-wall.md) gives an exact primality separator with sharp composite gap $(2+\sqrt2)/4$, while the [projector boundary analysis](prime-distribution/prime-state-projector-boundary.md) proves that the macroscopic state-$1$, prime and composite sectors must be centered before taking the projector limit. The [critical heat criterion](theta-and-heat/critical-heat-lyapunov-exponent.md) identifies RH with a zero Lyapunov exponent for the centered prime heat source. The [reverse-scale heat audit](goldbach/centered-goldbach-reverse-scale-audit.md) gives the exact signed horizon innovation and an unconditional PNT-scale bound. The [causal renewal development](factorial-and-renewal/prime-division-renewal-lyapunov.md) gives zeta as the scale-shift symbol, while the [Euler-discrepancy audit](successor-and-division/prime-division-euler-discrepancy.md) proves its positive primitive and cell asymptotics, supplies the causal trace correction, and shows that the centered remainder changes sign. The [linked-cell audit](factorial-and-renewal/causal-renewal-euler-discrepancy-audit.md) retains both classical trace terms and the negative-time history correction and proves a finite-horizon weighted energy inequality; its first-cell oscillatory test excludes unrestricted coercivity. The [actual cosine audit](factorial-and-renewal/actual-renewal-memory-negative-cosine.md) gives an alternate analytic-tail and finite-sum certificate for the negative multiplier at frequency 12 already identified in the [actual packet autocorrelation](goldbach/actual-centered-packet-lag-autocorrelation.md). The latter now proves that every critical zero of multiplicity $r$ forces $\liminf_N\operatorname{Re}S_N(\gamma)/\log N\le-r$ for the complete actual centered prime-power packet, while its renewal multiplier is positive at that ordinate. A subpower bound for the same packet at any one fixed frequency is RH-equivalent, while this forced logarithmic excursion rules out boundedness at a critical-zero frequency. Neither statement signs the full packet work. The [two-moment cell refinement](factorial-and-renewal/renewal-bernoulli-cell-phase-cancellation.md) gives an explicit late-cell phase coefficient after Bernoulli cancellation; [RenewalBernoulliCellMoments.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/RenewalBernoulliCellMoments.lean) compiles its polynomial moment identities, while the Euler--Maclaurin asymptotic remains written. The [actual-response cell audit](theta-and-heat/actual-heat-renewal-cell-trace-audit.md) certifies a second-cell derivative sign change, corrects the original response's nonzero trace and growing causal-history forcing, and constructs a zero-trace baseline with the same RH-equivalent late-time response. Its forcing has the positive asymptotic $\tfrac12\log(2\pi)e^{-v/2}+O(e^{-v})$, but a uniform response bound remains open. Rational enclosures are checked by [actual_heat_cell_interval.py](../formalization/verification/actual_heat_cell_interval.py). [ActualHeatRenewalRectangle.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualHeatRenewalRectangle.lean) formalizes the finite rectangle-rule estimate used in the baseline construction; its infinite heat application remains written analysis. [CausalRenewalFiniteCells.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/CausalRenewalFiniteCells.lean) compiles the cell factorization, square-root cell inequalities, finite linked-delay identity, and abstract energy lower bound with analytic Euler inputs as hypotheses. The source-specific phase/coercivity estimate remains unproved.

The [critical mean-square law](goldbach/centered-goldbach-heat-critical-mean-square-law.md) sharpens the centered heat criterion: RH is equivalent to $\mathscr A(T)=O(T)$, and under RH the exact limit $\mathscr A(T)/T=\sum_\rho m_\rho^2|\Gamma(\rho)|^2+o(1)$ holds without zero-spacing or linear-independence assumptions. The linear upper bound is not established unconditionally; the contour proof is written, not Lean-formalized.

The [critical heat criterion](theta-and-heat/critical-heat-lyapunov-exponent.md) now identifies its unconditional exponential growth rate exactly as $2\Theta-1$, where $\Theta$ is the supremum of real parts of nontrivial zeta zeros. The [finite centered Goldbach identity](goldbach/centered-goldbach-heat-lyapunov.md) gives the same exponent directly from its complete signed additive coefficient sum. The [complete-Lambda near-collision audit](goldbach/goldbach-heat-versus-buchstab-near-collision.md) also retains a positive heat cutoff: its uncentered integral diverges as the cutoff tends to zero.

The same actual-response audit proves the unconditional heat moment
$\int_0^\infty C(t)\,dt=-(1+\gamma)$. Thus its repaired causal response
has zero weighted total moment and the continuum renewal term is exactly
a future tail. The finite second-order trapezoid bound is formalized in
[ActualHeatRenewalRectangle.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ActualHeatRenewalRectangle.lean);
the Mellin moment and infinite-tail passage remain written proofs.
Landau's positive-Laplace theorem and the actual zeta poles show that
this response takes both signs arbitrarily late despite its eventually
positive forcing. More strongly, either eventual one-sided finite
bound on the response is RH-equivalent: if RH fails, the response is
unbounded in both directions. These written implications rule out
response positivity as the missing source-specific mechanism while
leaving a one-sided arithmetic bound as a precise target.

## Discrete harmonic convolution, regularity and aliasing

The [written proofs](analytic-methods/harmonic-convolution.md) give a general cancellation mechanism for the exact integer harmonic operator. Mean-square translation continuity yields a bilinear mean theorem, with spectral conditions allowing continuous and mixed spectra. A separate exponential-variation theorem permits escaping frequencies, while an exact counterexample shows that bounded mean energy and a vanishing primitive alone do not suffice. The arithmetic application retains every dilation and prime power and gives an alternative proof of the known RH-conditional leading term of $W$. Its general lemmas are unconditional; the arithmetic mean-energy input remains unproved. These results are not formalized in Lean.

## Harmonic prime Laplacian and infinite flow

The [phase and flow theorem](prime-distribution/harmonic-prime-laplacian-flow.md) proves a physical $1/p$ Laplacian lower bound on every fixed exponential frequency band. It constructs an unconditional infinite-prime divergence for the actual mean-zero line-one error, with finite edge energy, absolute weighted flux and admission error $O(\log P/\sqrt P)$. The complete prime-factorization inequality, its exact normalized harmonic average, the lcm formula and the closed bound for its prime weights are formalized in the [four linked Lean modules](prime-distribution/harmonic-prime-laplacian-flow.md#formalized-factorization-and-scope). The frequency lower bound and infinite-flow statements are written proofs.

## Retained ages, prime histories and source completion

The [age-resolved lift](prime-history/age-resolved-prime-history-lift.md) keeps the complete cell profile, exact successor wraps, prime-power seams and finite initial-age terms. Its analytic-radius equivalence restates the classical RH error criterion in these coordinates. The [two-prime unilateral theorem](prime-distribution/two-prime-unilateral-boundary.md) proves both compact boundary identities, including the common-resonance value and derivative constraints and the actual positive forward normalization.

The [causal-history construction](prime-history/causal-history-overflow.md) gives exact finite-band overflow with coherent interference, both physical ports and the unitary age-cell dictionary. Its [geometric entropy theorem](prime-history/complete-prime-history-entropy.md) quantifies the omitted averaged history tail. The independent [gamma-freezing proof](analytic-methods/uniform-full-product-arch-freezing.md) controls the full-product clipping error uniformly in the prime set, while retaining its unsigned arithmetic boundary.

For the actual prime-error source, [late-age transfer](prime-distribution/late-age-prime-boundary-transfer.md) proves deterministic operator estimates and a PNT-weighted completion of both boundary ports. The [Vinogradov–Korobov refinement](prime-history/late-age-vk-history-entropy.md) extends completion to all admitted primes through $P=o(R^{3/5}/(\log R)^{1/5})$ and proves trace-norm stability of the associated two-port Gram operator. The [successor compatibility theorem](successor-and-division/successor-collective-prime-compatibility.md) gives an exact compressed affine relation, the complete collective boundary seed and cutoff-uniform actual-source commutator estimates. These are written proofs. Separate positive port energies do not establish the signed two-history readout or the fixed-observation RH norm bound.

## Local poles at zeta zeros

[ZetaPole.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/ZetaPole.lean) proves that if $\zeta(\rho)=0$ and $\rho\ne0,1$, there are a positive integer $m$ and a function $a$ analytic at $\rho$ such that, on a punctured neighborhood,

```math
-\frac{\zeta'(s)}{s\zeta(s)}-\frac{1}{s-1}+\frac{2\gamma}{s}
=\frac{-m/\rho}{s-\rho}+a(s).
```

The residue is $-m/\rho\ne0$, including at multiple zeros. This identifies the singularity that remains after cancellation of a shared reciprocal-zeta term in prime-error formulas. Controlling the remaining arithmetic expression is still necessary to locate the zeros.

The result uses classical analytic zero factorization and logarithmic derivatives; see [Apostol's explicit formula, DLMF 25.16.2](https://dlmf.nist.gov/25.16.E2). Formal dependencies include mathlib's [isolated-zero factorization](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/f897ebcf72cd16f89ab4577d0c826cd14afaafc7/Mathlib/Analysis/Analytic/IsolatedZeros.lean) and [logarithmic derivative rules](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/f897ebcf72cd16f89ab4577d0c826cd14afaafc7/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/LogDeriv.lean).

[Verification](../formalization/verification/zeta-pole.json).

## Explicit floor correction

Define

```math
\begin{aligned}
D(q)&=\sum_{k=1}^{q}\left\lfloor\frac{q}{k}\right\rfloor,\\
H(q)&=\log(q!)-D(q)+2\gamma q,\\
\Delta(x)&=D(\lfloor x\rfloor)-x\log x-(2\gamma-1)x,\\
c_0&=\tfrac12\log(2\pi)-\gamma.
\end{aligned}
```

[FloorCorrection.lean](../formalization/BuildingBlocks/FloorCorrection.lean) proves, for every real $x\ge1$,

```math
\left|H(\lfloor x\rfloor)+\Delta(x)-c_0
+\left(\{x\}-\tfrac12\right)(\log x+2\gamma)\right|
\le\frac{2}{x}.
```

The bound includes integers, where $`\{x\}=0`$. It separates a constant mean from a weighted sawtooth, allowing Fourier estimates to be applied. On an integer block $B<n\le2B$ with $2B\le N$, the residuals at $x=N/n$ sum absolutely to at most $4B^2/N$. This block consequence is a written deduction; the formal theorem is the pointwise bound.

The mathematics follows from the [Stirling expansion and remainder estimates](https://dlmf.nist.gov/5.11). The imported mathlib [Stirling module](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/f897ebcf72cd16f89ab4577d0c826cd14afaafc7/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Stirling.lean) credits Moritz Firsching, Fabian Kruse, and Nikolas Kuhn; the [Euler–Mascheroni module](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/f897ebcf72cd16f89ab4577d0c826cd14afaafc7/Mathlib/NumberTheory/Harmonic/EulerMascheroni.lean) credits David Loeffler. Mathlib dependencies retain their Apache 2.0 license.

[Verification](../formalization/verification/floor-correction.json).
